# Harness notebook

Lab notebook of the harness agent (branch `agent/harness`). After a compaction: read `assets-src/shared/HARNESS-AGENT.md` again, then this file from the top.

## Where things are

- My clone: `/home/user/workspace/repo`, branch `agent/harness`. A second clone that plays the worker machine in tests: `/home/user/workspace/worker`.
- Files I own: `tools/round.py`, `tools/pack.ts`, this notebook. One new file under `tools/`: `tools/scratch_answers.py` (makes up critics' answers for scratch rounds, named in every report).
- Nothing under `src/`, `public/`, `config/` or `tools/lib/` is changed.
- Tests use the draft tier only.

## Facts read from the repo (2026-11-20, main at 7ba15bc)

- 46 segments, 47 judged frames. Fast kinds (`motion.fastKinds`): dive, rise, travel, retreat. Samples per frame: draft 1 and 1, review 4 and 16, final 16 and 64.
- The whole film pack of Phase 5 needs these rendered frames. Final tier: the 47 judged frames, the middle frame of `ending.fade` (the whole film sheets show one frame per segment, 46 in 4 sheets) and 7 more frames between 0 and 60 for the hook (9 hook frames, 0 and 60 are judged). Review tier: 12 frames for each of 8 travel segments and 3 ending segments.
- Cost on a worker, from the brief: a slow final still 40 to 95 s, a slow review frame 10 to 25 s. That is about 4.3 s per sample. A fast frame costs four times a slow one at the same tier (`kit/PROGRESS.md`, Render budget, Mars).
- `pack.ts` before my change: `--only` exists for one stop (`part()`): the word `stills` renders the stop's judged frames into `out/work/stills/`, a segment id renders its motion sheet into the round's cache, `ID-cover` its cover sheet. The hook rendered its 7 middle frames into a temp folder that is deleted, so a later run rendered them again.
- `stress.ts` writes `out/work/stress/<segment>-before-NNNNN.png`, `-after-NNNNN.png` and `<segment>.json` (a record for the segment). The PNG files have no record of their own.
- `round.py` before my change handles only `R3-part1` and `R3-part2`. The Sun has 5 judged frames (0, 60, terrain, rise, hold), which makes 3 parts. Reported to the Builder.
- A draft still of `mars.hold` takes 18 s here with the set load (4 cores).

## Decisions

1. Scratch mode (`--scratch`, round above 900) swaps two paths and switches git off: the ledger is a copy of `kit/PROGRESS.md` at `out/work/rounds/scratch/PROGRESS.md`, the scorecards go to `out/work/rounds/scratch/scorecards/`. Every other line of code is the same as in a real round, so the scratch run tests the real code. A round above 900 without `--scratch` is refused, and `--scratch` with a round up to 900 is refused.
2. Tiers below final are allowed in scratch mode only (`--tier draft`); the tool then adds `--tier draft --motion-tier draft` to every pack command and `--tier draft` to stress and measure.
3. Whole film part names for `npm run pack -- --phase 5|6 --round N --only ...` (no `--stop`): `stills:A-B` (every final tier frame of the whole film pack with A <= frame <= B: judged frames, whole film sheet frames, hook frames, all kept in `out/work/stills/`), `film-K` (whole film sheet K), `holds` (the grid of judged hold frames), `hook` (the phone size hook images), a segment id (its motion sheet), `ID-cover`. With `--stop` the old stop names keep their meaning.
4. The record round's pack (Phase 6) publishes one folder per stop and critic (`R1-mars`, `R3-mars-part1`, `R4-mars`), the whole film folders without a stop (`R3-part1`, `R4-part1`, `R5`, `R6`, `R7-part1`, `R2-part1`, `R8-part1`). Each R2 image is judged once; a stop's R2 is tallied from the votes on its own two frames and two controls, the whole film R2 from all of them.
5. Jobs are packed by expected minutes from a cost model (samples per frame times `--sample-seconds`, default 4.3) to at most `--job-minutes` (default 15) and spread over `--workers` (default 8); a worker gets several jobs in a row.
6. The record round collects its controls itself by default; `--controls-from N` reuses those of round N.
7. In whole film and record rounds a failed R2 is counted as one major issue in the round line, as the kit says. The stop round's line is left as it was.
8. A stop round with three or more R3 parts (the Sun: frames 0, 60, terrain, rise, hold): the extra parts are added after the four old critics, so R1, R3 part 1, R3 part 2 and R4 keep the families they had; `close` reads every part.
9. `close --vote NAME=LETTER` in a whole film or record round is kept in the round's state (`votes`) and is applied again by every later `close`; a round that reuses the controls of another round takes that round's corrections with them. The stop round is as before (the correction holds for that one call).
10. In the record round the families turn by stop: the critic at seat i of stop number s (Sun is 1) gets `SLOTS[(i + round + s) % 4]`, as if the stops had been judged in rounds that follow each other. The whole film critics get `SLOTS[(i + round) % 4]`, as in a whole film round.
11. A merge commit of mine is written with `git merge --no-ff -m` and carries the trailer. Two early ones (8ca6364, 7bbddc7) do not; they were pushed and stay.

## How to run (one line each)

- Render named parts of the whole film pack on a worker: `npm run pack -- --phase 5 --round N --only stills:0-1500,film-1,hook` (parts: `stills:A-B`, `film-K`, `holds`, `hook`, a segment id; in Phase 6 also `ID-cover`). The last line says whether a render session was opened.
- The list of frames and sheets a whole film pack needs, with nothing rendered: `npm run -s pack -- --phase 5 --round N --plan`.
- Whole film round: `python3 tools/round.py start --round N --whole`, then `jobs`, `place`, `pack`, `votes`, `close`, each with `--round N`. After `start` the tool knows the round's kind from its state.
- Record round: `python3 tools/round.py start --round N --record`, then the same five steps. `pack --controls-from M` reuses the controls of round M.
- Scratch: add `--scratch` to every step and use a round above 900; `start` also takes `--tier draft --every 10 --allow-red`. Made up answers: `python3 tools/scratch_answers.py --round N | python3 tools/round.py votes --round N --scratch`.
- `jobs` takes `--workers 8 --job-minutes 15 --sample-seconds 4.3 --measure-minutes 20`.

## Part 3 (mail of 2026-11-21): master.py, cues.ts, gauntlet.py

From this mail on `tools/round.py` and `tools/pack.ts` are the Builder's again: I do not edit them. Part 3 is new files only, one script line per tool in `package.json`: `tools/master.py` (`npm run master`), `tools/cues.ts` (`npm run cues`), `tools/gauntlet.py` (`npm run gauntlet`).

### Decisions

1. `master.py` keeps a plan in `out/work/master/<name>/` (`plan.json`, `jobs.json`, `state.json`); the name is `<format id>-<tier>` unless `--name` is given; `out/work/master/current` names the last plan.
2. Cost model: samples per frame of the tier (the fast kinds use `fastSamplesPerFrame`) times seconds per sample (4.3 in master, times 0.45 in preview, times 3.5 in uhd, from the Mars budget table; `--sample-seconds` sets it) plus `--overhead-minutes` per job. A segment is cut into equal spans no longer than the job minutes; spans are grouped into jobs in film order; jobs go to the least loaded worker, longest first.
3. A tier fits when the busiest worker's hours are within the limit (`sandbox.sandboxMasterMaxHours`, or `previewFilmMaxHours` with `--preview`); the machine hours of all workers are printed too. `--fit` tries final, then review; for the master the floor is review, for `--preview` it is draft, because kit/05 says never below draft.
4. `place` matches attachments by file name (`SEG.spanA-B.mp4` with `.mp4.json`), checks the record (engine hash, the hashes of the stops the segment shows, tier, format, full assets, beauty, first and last frame) and the file with ffprobe (frames, size, fps, codec), and copies good pairs into the chunk folder. Attachments are never deleted. Records of another build are listed and not used.
5. `join` refuses when the tree's hashes differ from the plan, because `film.ts` deletes chunks of another build. It only runs the existing commands: `npm run film -- --segment IDS` for segments whose spans are all here (no frame is rendered, the tool says so), `npm run film -- --join`, `npm run clip -- --stop S`.
6. `cues.ts` reads the intro times from the intro records (`out/posts/intro-<stop>.mp4.json`, else `out/work/posts/`). With no record the posts table says so and `--check` prints a note, not a failure. `--check` compares `cut/CUES.md` with what would be written now, the rows of `cut/SHOTS.md` with `segments`, and `cut/SHOTS.md` with a fresh run of `tools/shots.ts` in a temporary folder.
7. I do not commit a `cut/CUES.md`: it is the Builder's Phase 6 step 9 output and the intro records are not on main yet.
8. `gauntlet.py` writes no file. By default it runs only quick checks (searches, ffprobe, frame comparisons, `npm run cues -- --check`). The slow ones are asked for with `--run gate,clone,subfolder`; they write only under `out/work/` and `dist/` and in a temporary folder. Without `--run gate` it reads the last `out/work/gate/phase-6.json` and says NOT RUN when that is older than the last commit.
9. Rules the gauntlet tool sets itself and prints with the result: no fade in means frame 0 has at least 90 percent of the mean luma of frames 1 to 30; black means no value above 2 of 255 in a film file and 4 in a kept JPEG; a post that was encoded once needs at least 40 dB at the cut and must be closer to frame 0 of the clip than to frame 1 or to the intro (`--cut-db`).
10. The tool holds a list of family names to search the interface and the README for; it also takes the names in `scorecards/*/agents.json`. The list is in a tool, not in the interface.

### How to run

- `npm run master -- plan --tier review --workers 8` (or `--fit`, `--preview`, `--stops mars`, `--segments mars.travel`, `--job-minutes 15`, `--sample-seconds 4.3`, `--name N`, `--replace`), then `jobs`, send each message of `jobs.json` to its worker, `place` when files are back, `status` to see a stuck worker, `jobs --missing [--to 3]` to send lost spans again, `join [--stops a,b] [--film]`.
- `npm run cues` writes `cut/CUES.md`; `npm run cues -- --check` checks it and `cut/SHOTS.md`.
- `npm run gauntlet` prints the block for "QA results"; `--run gate,clone,subfolder` or `--run all`, `--items 6,21,P`, `--deep`, `--full`, `--offline`, `--pass N`.

### Tars for master jobs (mail of 2026-11-21 evening)

- The job message of `master.py` now asks for one tar per job from the repo root (`tar -cf /tmp/<job>.tar <files>`, `split -b 40m -d /tmp/<job>.tar /tmp/<job>.tar.`), the pieces as attachments, at most 10 per message, with the one line `JOB <job> done`. The sentence about exit code 3 is kept. `plan`, `jobs.json`, `state.json` and `join` are as before.
- `scan()` finds `film-*.tar.NN` pieces anywhere under `~/.perplexity/attachments/` whose name is a job of the plan (or that name with `-againN`), takes the newest file per piece number, and reads the pieces in order as one stream. A tar counts as complete when its piece numbers have no gap and it ends with the 1024 zero bytes that end a tar; otherwise it is named in one line (`tars whose pieces are not all here yet`) and looked at again on the next scan. Only regular members whose base name is a span file (`SEG.spanA-B.mp4`, `.mp4.json`) are taken, by base name, into `out/work/master/<plan>/tars/<job>/`; no path from the tar is used, links are ignored. From there they go through the same checks as loose attachments and are moved into the chunk folder; a `pieces.json` there keeps a tar from being unpacked twice. Loose span files work as before.
- Test with a scratch plan `tartest` (the 16x9-draft plan under another name, its chunk folder and loose attachments moved aside and put back after): job 005 packed by the message's commands with 5m pieces, two of three pieces there: `place` 0 placed, exit 0, the tar named as not complete; third piece: 2 spans placed; job 001 by the exact commands (one 40m piece) and job 003 as loose files: 2 more placed; the three spans from tars are byte identical to the worker's files; a tar `film-tartest-001-again1` with the pair of the old build: not placed, listed under records from another build (engine a7e2e4a9a2b7be97, the plan has 704d5d55f51b7b15); a tar whose members are named `../../../../../../tmp/escape/...`, an absolute path and two links: nothing written outside, the span placed by its base name, no link; a tar cut in the middle: named as not complete; a tar of another plan's name: not looked at; `place` again: 0 new.
- Not covered: two different tars of the same job name that arrive mixed (newest file per piece number wins; the record and ffprobe checks then decide); piece numbers above 9899.

## MAIL 93 (2026-11-22): faults found by the Phase 6 rehearsal

Mine from this mail on: `tools/proof.ts`, `tools/review.ts`, `tools/gate.ts` (G9), `package.json` scripts, besides `tools/master.py`, `tools/cues.ts`, `tools/gauntlet.py`. Still not mine: `tools/round.py`, `tools/pack.ts`, `tools/lib/`. A clock job runs beside it: one `TICK HH:MM UTC` to `/root` every 10 minutes through 11:52 UTC, then `TICKS DONE`.

- `tools/proof.ts`, memory rule: the total after the travel is held against the arriving set, which is the peak (both sets) less the set before the travel, with the same 5 percent. When no sample holds both sets the error says so. The PSNR rule is untouched (30 dB; the Builder cures the cause in `src/capture/export.ts`).
- The proof's record has carried `pass` and `errors` in `extra` since the first proof command; G9 (`tools/gate.ts`) and gauntlet item 15 now read them and go red when the newest proof did not pass. Item 16 already read `pass` of `memory.json`.
- `tools/review.ts`: order is the nine posts (from `out/work/posts`, then `out/posts`; a smaller copy at the preview size when over `repo.maxFileMb`), the preview film, the nine clips, the comparison images (hold, then terrain), all in stop order with no file time, then the rest newest first as before. The pin of the current stop is gone because the groups hold it.
- `package.json`: script `shots`. `npm run cues -- --check` names the stale or missing file and the command that remakes it.
- `tools/cues.ts`: the posts table names the record by file name only, so the sheet is the same before and after `npm run keep -- --posts` moves the intros.

## MAIL 150 (2026-11-22 12:16 UTC): polish mode of the round tools

`tools/round.py` and `tools/pack.ts` are mine again for this task. New files: `tools/select.ts` (the selection as a pure function, so a test can hold it), `tests/select.test.ts`. A clock job runs beside it: `TICK HH:MM UTC` every 10 minutes through 17:52 UTC, then `TICKS DONE` at 18:00.

Decisions:

1. `FILM['polish'] = (6, 'polish', 'round', 'polishMaxRounds')`; `start --polish` is `film_start` with that entry. It also writes `out/work/rounds/round-N/stress-base.json`, the pixel hashes of the before stills in `out/work/stress` as they are before the round (the kept ones); `place` writes it too when it is not there yet.
2. `jobs` in a polish round is the record round's list. `--stops a,b` limits the measures and the stop sheets; `--no-stress` leaves the stress jobs out. The plan is asked for with `--plan --judged`, which adds the judged frames with their segment, stop, kind and reason to `plan.json`; without `--judged` the plan is what it was.
3. `touched --round N --base FILE` hashes with `hashlib.sha256(Image.open(p).convert('RGB').tobytes()).hexdigest()[:16]`. A changed final still that is a judged frame goes to its stop (kinds hold, dive, terrain, rise) or to the film (travels, ending). R1 and R8 of a stop are true when the judged frame of its hold or of its terrain changed (for the Sun that is frames 90 and 360; frames 0 and 60 are judged frames of `sun.terrain` and go to R3 only). R5 is true when a changed still is a frame of a whole film sheet or of the holds sheet, or when a `whole-film-*` or `whole-holds-*` image changed; R6 when a hook frame or a `hook-*` image changed. `motion-<id>.png` gives the sheet id. R7 lists a stress segment when one of its before stills differs from `stress-base.json` (or from a `stress` map in FILE when there is one) or has no hash there. A name with no hash in FILE counts as changed and is listed under `unknown`, with one exception: the images the pack itself makes from the stills (`detail-`, `match-`, `jury-`, `whole-jury-`, `whole-control-`) are listed under `unknown` but switch nothing when FILE has no hash for them, because their stills are compared themselves; when FILE has a hash and it differs they count (detail to its frame, match to R1, jury to R8).
4. `pack.ts --phase 6 --round N --select FILE` builds what `polishBuilds()` of `tools/select.ts` lists, in this order: per stop R1, R3, R4 (folders `R1-venus`, `R3-venus`, `R4-venus`, with `-partK` when the images need it), `R8` over the stops with R8 true, `R2`, `R7`, `R6`, `R5`, then `R3` and `R4` of the film. R2: when the film's R2 is true the whole film jury is built (it holds every stop's two frames and two controls, so a stop's R2 is tallied from it); otherwise one `R2` folder with the Phase 4 stop jury (hold and terrain, each with its approved image) of the stops with R2 true, shuffled. A frame, sheet, stop or stress segment the build does not know stops the pack with one line. Folders of an earlier run that are no longer selected are removed by the rule that was there.
5. `close` in a polish round: the cards, the jurors' file, the key and the manifest as in the other film rounds; `SUMMARY.md` with the build line, the fixed sentence, what was judged and what was not (from `select.json` against the full list of the plan), the measures of the stops whose measure is from this build, one score row per critic and scope that was asked, R2 and R8 tables only where asked, the issues worst first, the jurors' words, what changes next. No pass bar is computed; `holds` is not written into the state. A failed R2 counts as one major, as in the other film rounds. The round row gets ` Judged on main at <sha>, only what the batch touched. <scores>. S severe, M major. Next: ... `scorecards/round-N/`.`
6. Controls of R2 and R8 in a polish round are judged in the round unless `pack --controls-from M` names a round whose `jurors.txt` holds them.

7. MAIL 152: `start --polish --row-only` writes the row, the status line, the commit "Round N starts: Phase 6, polish, round k of 3" and the state, and stops before the checks. A later `start --polish` finds the row, asks for no clean tree and does not merge origin/main, runs the machine checks on the tree as it is, commits through `commit()` and records commit, engine hash and stop hashes. `--row-only` on a round that has its row, or without `--polish`, is refused.
8. `close` in a polish round deletes card files of critics that are no longer in the selection (left by an earlier close of the same round).

How to run a polish round (round 33): `python3 tools/round.py start --round 33 --polish --row-only`, merge the batch, `start --round 33 --polish`, `jobs --round 33 --workers 40 [--stops a,b] [--no-stress]`, `place --round 33`, `touched --round 33 --base FILE`, edit `scorecards/round-033/select.json`, `pack --round 33 [--controls-from M]`, `votes --round 33`, `close --round 33 --next "..."`.

## MAIL 161 (2026-11-22 13:28 UTC): start builds first, the checks follow

Only `tools/round.py` changed. For polish rounds and the record round; whole film rounds and stop rounds start as before.

### Decisions

1. `start` has two steps. The build step: a dirty tree is committed without a push ("Round N: polish, the tree at the build"), `origin/main` is fetched and merged, `npm run -s build` runs (the same command as G1), the hashes come from `ensureBuild()` of `tools/lib/common.ts` (called, not changed) through one `npx tsx -e` line, the state gets `commit`, `engineHash`, `stopHashes`, they are printed, and the commit is pushed. The checks step: the build once more, the hashes of the tree against the state, the tests, the gate with the full machine list (so `gate.txt` has all eleven lines as before), `gateGreen` into the state.
2. Which step runs: `--build-only` runs the build step and returns; `--checks` runs the checks step and refuses when the state has no build; a plain call runs the build step when the state has no build and then the checks step, so a plain second call after `--build-only` only checks.
3. "Moved" is decided by the hashes of the tree, not by the commit: the state's engine hash and stop hashes against what the tree gives now. Commits that touch logs, scorecards, kit files or tools give the same hashes and do not trip it. When it trips, the tool stops in one line and names the hashes; `--build-only` takes the new tree, and when the hashes changed it drops `gateGreen` and `gate.txt`.
4. `pack` (polish and record) refuses while the state has no `gateGreen`, and refuses when the tree's hashes are not the state's. A red gate with `--allow-red` writes `gateGreen` false and the pack runs, as before.
5. The state's commit on origin: after the build step and after the checks step the tool fetches, and when the state's commit is not an ancestor of `origin/main` it merges and pushes (no force); if it still is not there it prints "commit X of the state is not on origin/main: push it before the jobs go out", and the last line of `--build-only` then reads "no jobs before the push".
6. `start` without `--polish` or `--record` takes the mode from the state when the round has one.

### How to run

- `python3 tools/round.py start --round N --polish --row-only`, merge the batch, `start --round N --polish --build-only`, `jobs --round N --workers 40`, `start --round N --checks`, then `place`, `touched`, `pack`, `votes`, `close` as before.
- One call does both: `start --round N --polish [--allow-red]` prints the build line, then runs the checks; `jobs` can run from a second shell after the build line.

### Proofs

- Real mode in a scratch clone (the second clone with a bare copy of the repo as its origin, `scratch/origin.git`; the gate and `proof` answered by a stand in script on the PATH so one call takes a minute; the build, the hashes and the tests were real). Round 34: `--row-only` wrote the row and pushed; a made up batch commit (one line in `src/stops/mercury/assets.ts`), not pushed; `--build-only` printed commit, engine and nine stop hashes (Mercury's changed), and origin's main was at the state's commit after it; `pack` refused in one line; `jobs --workers 40` gave 77 jobs at once. A commit that touched `kit/PROGRESS.md`, a scorecards file and this notebook, then `--checks`: green, state commit unchanged. A commit in `src/core`: `--checks` and the plain call both stopped with "the tree moved since the build at 3855f411: engine f92f... is now 930c..."; `pack` stopped the same way; `--build-only` took the new commit and dropped `gateGreen` and `gate.txt`; the plain second call checked only. A commit pushed to origin from a third clone while this tree was dirty: the dirty tree was committed, origin merged, built, pushed. Gate red: no `gateGreen`, pack refused; with `--allow-red` `gateGreen` false. Push refused by a hook in the origin: the line "commit ... is not on origin/main" and "no jobs before the push"; the next `--checks` pushed it.
- Record mode in the same clone: round 36 `--record --build-only` (row, build, state), pack refused, plain second call checked; round 37 `--record` in one call.
- Refusals: `--build-only` or `--checks` on a whole film round or a stop round, `--checks` with no build, two of the three flags.
- Scratch round 941 in this repo at the review tier, real gate: `start --round 941 --polish --scratch --tier review --every 30 --build-only` (11 s), `jobs --workers 40 --stops mercury --no-stress` at once (19 jobs), `pack` refused, `start --checks` (the full gate, 18 min, green), a hand written `select.json` (Mercury R1 and R8), `pack` (7 critics), made up answers, `close`: SUMMARY with the checks line from `gate.txt`, row "R1 mercury 9, R8 mercury fail. 0 severe, 0 major".
- Tests and build: `npx vitest run` 17 files 87 tests, `npm run -s build` clean, `pyflakes` clean.

### A fault found on the way, in `tools/pack.ts`

The last line of a pack ("frames were rendered" or "nothing was rendered, no render session was opened") looked for the render session in `capture.session`. Since main's 5c16a2cc (2026-11-21 17:39) `tools/lib/render.ts` keeps it in `capture.held.session`, so from then on the line said "nothing was rendered" every time. Seen here: the pack of round 941 rendered frames 615 and 870 at review (the two stills have its time) and printed "nothing was rendered". Fixed: the line reads both places. What this touches in older proofs: the byte comparisons of part 1 (2026-11-21 05:46, before that change) stand; the line quoted for scratch round 931 today proves nothing by itself. What does prove it there, checked after the fact: no draft still and no motion sheet of round 931 has a file time after `place` (the last sheet 13:04:54, placed 13:05:03, pack after that).

## MAIL 162 (2026-11-22 13:36 UTC): a long sheet split by frames

Changed: `tools/pack.ts`, `tools/round.py`. Nothing under `tools/lib/`.

### Decisions

1. A motion sheet was already put together from one still per frame by `Capture.sheet` of `tools/lib/render.ts`, which takes a still from the pack's temp folder when its record matches. So a tile is that still, kept: `npm run pack -- --phase P --round N [--stop ID] --only <part> --frames A-B` renders the frames of that sheet whose number lies in A to B into `out/work/packs/.cache/round-NNN/tiles/<part>/f<frame>-<tier>.png`, each with its record (engine hash, stop hashes, tier, frame). `--frames` goes with `--only` and one sheet.
2. The join: when a sheet is not in the cache and its tiles folder is there, the pack checks every tile's record against this build; when all match it copies them into the temp folder and calls the same `Capture.sheet`, which then composes and renders nothing. When one is missing or from another build the pack stops with "sheet <part>: no tile of this build for frames ...; nothing rendered". With no tiles folder the pack behaves as before.
3. `--join` (no `--only`): joins every split sheet whose tiles are complete, prints "sheet <part>: joined from 12 tiles" or "sheet <part>: not joined, no tile of this build for frames ...", publishes nothing and writes no key. `round.py place` calls it after unpacking when the cache has a tiles folder, so `touched` finds the joined sheet under its usual name.
4. `--plan` gives each sheet its tiles as `[frame, samples]`. `round.py jobs` (whole film, record and polish rounds; not the stop rounds of Phase 4) cuts a job of one sheet whose estimate is over `--job-minutes` into runs of frames under it, named `<name>-a`, `-b`, and so on, same command plus `--frames A-B`, same tar. A single frame over the minutes stays one job.
5. `place` checks the tiles' records too in its "records from another build" line.

### Proofs

- Review tier, `mars.dive-cover` (12 frames 2449 to 2460, 192 samples): one job into the cache of scratch round 951 (405.6 s, "frames were rendered"); two halves `--frames 2449-2454` and `--frames 2455-2460` as two processes into round 952 (178.5 s and 260.3 s), then `--join` ("joined from 12 tiles", "nothing was rendered", 1.8 s). Both sheets: pixel hash 912a8d38b48270fb, file sha256 1910a59ca3c03ea7, 1400 by 1156, and equal records.
- One tile moved away: `--join` said "frames 2457" and rendered nothing in 0.0 s; the pack with `--only mars.dive-cover` stopped with the error line. One tile's record given another engine hash: named the same way.
- The joined sheet deleted, then a plain `pack --only mars.dive-cover` with no `--frames`: joined inside the pack, "nothing was rendered", same file sha256.
- The round tool, scratch polish round 943 at draft on ca8a73c5: `jobs --workers 40 --stops mars --no-stress --job-minutes 0.3` cut the four Mars dive and rise sheets into `-a`, `-b`, `-c` each; the second clone ran those 12 jobs and the views job (34 to 40 s each); `place` half way joined `mars.dive` and said "sheet mars.dive-cover: not joined, no tile of this build for frames 2453, ... 2460"; `place` at the end joined the other three, "nothing was rendered"; `touched` against a base with two sheet hashes changed by hand named `mars.dive-cover` and `mars.rise`; `start --checks` (real gate, green), `pack` (R4 mars, 2 images, nothing rendered, 0.1 s), `close`.
- No change where nothing is split: `--plan --judged` of phases 5 and 6 is the old plan plus `tiles`, and each sheet's tiles sum to its samples; `jobs --job-minutes 200` writes the same `jobs.json` as the tool before this change. Tests 17 files 87, build clean.
- Real tiers, plan only (state of a polish round, `jobs --workers 40`): 192 jobs in place of 77, longest job 20.0 min (a stop's measure, a fixed estimate), longest render job 17.2 min (the stress clips, `r34-film-stress02`), longest sheet job 15.0 min, 48 to 54 min per worker in place of 43 to 97. The Sun's rise cover is 11 jobs of 5.9 to 13.8 min. With `--job-minutes 20`: 138 jobs; with 30: 103 jobs, longest 30.0.

What the tool calls the frozen build: the engine hash and the nine stop hashes that `start --round N --record --build-only` writes into `out/work/rounds/round-NNN/state.json`. They cover `src/`, `public/assets/`, `config/` and `package-lock.json` (`tools/lib/hashes.ts`). There is no freeze command. `start --checks` and `pack` exit with "the tree moved since the build" when they differ, `master join` exits when the tree differs from its plan, and gauntlet item 7 compares the engine hash in the record round's SUMMARY.md with the tree.

Freeze and the renders (steps 2, 3 and the renders of step 4 are the same jobs):

1. `python3 tools/round.py start --round N --record --build-only`. Expects: the last polish round closed and pushed; `git status --porcelain` empty (an untracked file that is not ignored counts); in `kit/PROGRESS.md` the sections `## Rounds` and `## Final scores` and fewer than 2 lines `- Round X, Phase 6, record round,`. Writes the row "run 1 of 2" (commit, push to main), builds, writes `state.json` (commit, engineHash, stopHashes), prints "jobs can go out".
2. `python3 tools/round.py jobs --round N --workers 40 --job-minutes 20`. No `--stops` and no `--no-stress` in record mode (it exits). Expects the state of step 1. Writes `plan.json` and `jobs.json`: `rN-film-stillsKK` (the 55 final stills, which hold all 47 judged frames, and the whole film and hook sheets), `rN-film-motionKK`, `rN-film-stressKK` (4 segments), and per stop `rN-<stop>-measure`, `-views` (hold and terrain beside the approved image), `-dive`, `-dive-cover`, `-rise`, `-rise-cover`; a long sheet is split into `-a`, `-b` with `--frames`. Send each `message` to its worker.
3. `python3 tools/round.py start --round N --checks` while the workers render. Expects `engineHash` in the state and the tree unmoved. Build, tests, G9 (when red it runs `npm run -s proof` again on this build), the gate with `--phase 6 --only G1,G2,G3,G4,G6,G7,G10,G11,G12,G14,G15`; writes `gate.txt` and `gateGreen`, commits, pushes. No `place`, `jobs`, `pack`, `compare` or `keep` while it is in a build step.
4. `python3 tools/round.py place --round N`, as often as tars come. Expects `jobs.json` and the pieces `~/.perplexity/attachments/*/rN-*.tar.NN`. Until it prints "missing: none", "records from another build: none" and no "sheet X: not joined" line. Then on disk: `out/work/stills/f<frame>-16x9-final-full.png` and `.json` (55), `out/work/packs/.cache/round-NNN/*.png` and `.json`, `out/work/stress/<segment>-before-*.png`, `-after-*.png`, `<segment>.json` (4), `out/work/measure/<stop>.json` (9).
5. Both comparison images of every stop: `for s in sun mercury venus earth mars jupiter saturn uranus neptune; do for v in hold terrain; do npm run -s compare -- --stop $s --view $v; done; done`. Expects the placed final still of the stop's judged hold and terrain frame with a record of this build; then nothing is rendered and no browser opens (seconds per image). Without it `compare` renders that frame at the final tier on this VM. Also the approved images (`stop.hero`, `stop.close`), `dist/` of this tree, 3 GB free. Writes `out/work/compare/compare-<stop>-<view>.jpg` and `-view.jpg` with records.
6. `npm run -s keep`. Expects the tree at the frozen build. Copies what has a record of this build into `out/stills/`: `f<frame>-<segment>.jpg` for the 47 judged frames, `compare-<stop>-<view>.jpg` (18), `sheet-<segment>.jpg`, `sheet-<segment>-cover.jpg`, `sheet-film-NN.jpg`. Record round (step 4):

Expects `gateGreen` in the state (else "no pack before the machine checks"), the tree unmoved, `scorecards/round-030/jurors.txt`, the line `- Next critic name: reviewer_X` in PROGRESS (it moves it on and does not commit), every still, sheet and stress still placed. Its last line must say "nothing was rendered, no render session was opened"; "R7 skipped" means the stress stills are not there. Writes `out/work/packs/round-NNN/`, `out/work/packs/keys/round-NNN.json`, `critics.json`. No approved image goes to a juror.
8. Send every entry of `out/work/rounds/round-NNN/critics.json` to a fresh critic. A card critic attaches `<name>.json` (read from `~/.perplexity/attachments/*/<name>.json`); a juror (R2, R8) answers in text.
9. `python3 tools/round.py votes --round N < FILE`, one line `reviewer_X|answer` per juror; writes `answers/<name>.txt`; can be run again.
10. `python3 tools/round.py close --round N --next "..." --vote reviewer_15@3=a`. The record round judges R2 of all nine stops, so the mars correction is always needed. Expects: every answer (else "no answer yet" and nothing is written), `keys/round-NNN.json`, `gate.txt`, the nine `out/work/measure/<stop>.json` of this build (else "stale" or "not measured"), `scorecards/round-030/key.json` and `jurors.txt` (whole film R2), and key.json with jurors.txt of rounds 3 (mars), 6 (uranus), 7 (mercury), 8 (jupiter), 9 (neptune), 10 (sun), 11 (earth), 12 (venus), 13 (saturn) for each stop's R2 and R8 (all on main). Master, clips, preview, proof (steps 5 to 8). `tools/master.py` takes nothing from `round.py`; only the build must be the same:

11. `npm run master -- plan --fit --workers 40 --job-minutes 20` (or `--tier review`). Expects `git status --porcelain -- src public config package.json package-lock.json` empty and HEAD pushed. Writes `out/work/master/16x9-<tier>/plan.json`, `state.json` and `out/work/master/current`. Its line "plan <name>: commit X, engine Y" must show the engineHash of the record round's state. With 40 workers the final tier may fit the 24 hours; `--fit` takes the highest tier that fits.
12. `npm run master -- jobs`; send each `message` of `out/work/master/<name>/jobs.json`.
13. `npm run master -- place` as often as tars come (pieces `film-<name>-KKK.tar.NN` anywhere under the attachments) until "missing: none"; `npm run master -- status` shows a stuck worker; `npm run master -- jobs --missing --to 3,4` sends lost spans again.
14. `npm run master -- join`. Expects the tree at the plan's hashes (else it exits and joins nothing), every span placed, no file the plan does not know in `out/work/chunks/16x9-<tier>-full/`. Makes `out/work/film-16x9-<tier>.mp4` and the nine `out/work/clips/<stop>.mp4` with no frame rendered (a line "warning: frames were rendered here" says otherwise). Last line "join: every file asked for is made and passes its checks".

16. `npm run keep -- --clips`. Writes `out/preview.mp4` and `out/clips/<stop>.mp4`; it stops with "refusing" on a file over 95 MB.
17. `npm run proof`, then `npm run proof -- --memory` (draft tier fixed in the tool, rendered on this VM, one after the other). Writes `out/work/export/proof-preview.mp4` and `memory.json` with `pass` in the records.
18. `npm run gate -- --phase 6` (G8 reads `out/preview.mp4` and its record, G9 the proof, G16 `out/roughcut.mp4`) and `npm run gauntlet`.

## MAIL 323 (2026-11-23 18:32 UTC): the cover sheet of a stop round shows the place window

Fault: `sheetFrames(cfg, s, true)` took `judgedWindow` and showed T-5 to T+6, but the place window of a handover (the "window" of the handover report, `placeFirstFrame` to `placeLastFrame`) ends at T for a dive and starts at T for a rise, so the motion critic saw half of it.

Change, four files, nothing under `src/`:
- `tools/lib/render.ts` (used by the renderers, named in the report): `sheetFrames` has a fourth argument `placeWindow`, default false. With it, for a dive or rise of a stop with a handover, the sheet holds `critics.contactSheetFrames` consecutive frames centred on the middle of the place window, kept inside the segment. When the centre falls between two frames the run leans to the end where the sides change: the last frame of a dive's window, the first of a rise's. Without the argument, and for a stop with a cover, the frames are the old ones.
- `tools/pack.ts`: the flag `--place-window` is passed on to the three `sheetFrames(cfg, s, true)` calls (the sheet and the two in the plan).
- `tools/round.py`: the stop round's `jobs` and `pack` commands carry `--place-window`. `film_jobs`, `film_pack` and the `--plan` calls of the whole film rounds do not, so record and polish rounds keep their frames (item 2 of the mail, read by the letter). To give the whole film rounds the place window too, add the flag in `film_jobs` (the plan and `cmd`), `film_touched` and `film_pack`.
- `tests/coversheet.test.ts`: five tests (dive ending at T, rise starting at T, longer and shorter windows, the segment edge, the old path).

## MAIL 325 (2026-11-23 18:41 UTC): the place window in the whole film rounds too

The Builder's word on MAIL 323: the tie stands (a dive shows up to T, a rise from T on); my reading of item 2 was wrong, the whole film rounds must show the same cover frames as a stop round.

Change: `tiers(s, 'pack')` in `tools/round.py` now always starts with ` --place-window`, and the two explicit flags of MAIL 323 are gone, so all seven pack commands of the tool carry it: stop `jobs`, stop `pack`, `film_plan` (used by `film_touched`), the `--plan` of `film_jobs`, the job command `cmd` of `film_jobs`, the `--join` of `film_place`, and `film_pack` (through `extra`). `tools/pack.ts` and `tools/lib/render.ts` are as in MAIL 323. The flag only acts where a handover exists, so a stop with a cover keeps its frames in every round, and Phase 5 rounds have no cover sheets at all. Test: a sixth test in `tests/coversheet.test.ts` reads `tools/round.py` and checks that `tiers` gives the flag for the pack before it looks at the tier and that each of the seven pack commands goes through `tiers(s, 'pack')` or `extra`.

What follows in a polish round: `touched` compares `motion-<part>.png` by its pixels against the base, the name stays, so the cover sheets of the stops with a handover count as changed in the first polish round after this, which is true. A `plan.json` written by `film_plan` before this change keeps the old tile list of that round; the pack reads the frames live.

The rule when a place window is longer than the sheet: the sheet is still `critics.contactSheetFrames` frames, centred on the middle of the window, so neither end of the window is on it and T (which sits at the end of a dive's window and at the start of a rise's) is not shown. On main bab673f that is the Mars rise: window 2685 to 2711 (27 frames), sheet 2692 to 2703, T 2685 not on it. When a window is shorter than the sheet, the sheet reaches past both ends of it by the same number of frames, give or take the one frame of the tie. A window of 12 or 13 frames shows all of it, or all but the end frame away from T.

G13 and detail from functions (asked by `saturn_coder`, answered by mail, copy to the Builder): the texels view counts only the number the material hands to `diagOut`; it cannot see functions. A true report: the cells of the finest octave that is really drawn at that pixel, from the same expression and uniforms as the beauty path, counted only where the function detail has its weight (the Sun's ground switches at half weight), never a constant, and the counted flag left alone. The tools have no rule on contrast; G17 only looks at the judged hold and terrain frames, so the proof that the detail is seen is a native crop and a sharpness number from the coder, and the bar is the Builder's.

## Mail 328 (2026-11-23 19:07 UTC): a report beside G13, what the material says against what the picture carries

Asked: a test that holds the texels view against a sharpness measure of the nograin frame, as a report beside G13 (no threshold moves, no pass rule changes), per frame or frame list, on any commit, through `npm run measure` with a flag.

Built, four files, nothing under `src/`, `tools/lib/` untouched:
- `tools/measure/carried.ts` (new): `carried(texels, picture, minTexelsPerPixel)` and its definition `CARRIED` (`report-against-picture-v1`). Grid 32 by 18 (60 px tiles in the master). A pixel is used when it counts in the texels view and all pixels within 6 px count too, so limbs and matte edges add nothing; a tile is judged when half of its pixels are used. Per tile: the median report (red byte over 255 times 2, the view cannot say more than 2), the share of soft pixels, the mean luma, the energy of the band of G17 (sigma 0.6 minus 2.0, mean square), the energy of the band below (2.0 minus 6.0). Flag: says sharp (median at least `detail.minTexelsPerPixel`), not dark (luma above 0.04), energy under 2e-5 (the flat tile floor of G17). Also the tiles by band of the report (under 1.25, 1.25 to under 1.9, 1.9 and over).
- `tools/measure.ts`: `--carried FRAMES` (list and ranges), `--tier` for the picture (default review, texels always draft). It renders, prints, writes `out/work/measure/<stop>-carried.json` and the PNGs in `out/work/measure/<stop>/carried-NNNNN-*.png`, then leaves. The G5, G13, G17 path is as it was and `<stop>.json` is not written.
- `tools/measure/README.md`: a section on it. `tests/carried.test.ts`: six tests on synthetic pictures.

How to run it on another commit without merging: a worktree of that commit, `node_modules` linked, `tools/measure.ts` and `tools/measure/carried.ts` copied in (the tools of a284d546 were the same as main's), nothing committed there.

What the scale means (from the test picture, noise drawn at 1, 2, 4, 8, 16 px per cell with linear filtering): fine over coarse 13.6, 4.3, 0.68, 0.12, 0.05. A picture of nature with detail at every scale sits near 1. So a ratio near 1 is detail down to the pixel, a ratio near 0.1 is a map drawn eight times too large. The energy is contrast squared: 4e-5 is a band signal of 0.6 percent of full scale (rms), the floor 2e-5 is 0.45 percent.

First use, 19:11 to 19:41 UTC, one render at a time:
- Saturn, `agent/saturn-code` a284d546, frames 3804, 3810, 3815, 3820, 3828, draft (1.5 min) and review (12 min). G13: 0 soft pixels in all five, least 1.051, 1.012, 1.004, 1.004, 1.145. Globe tiles (report under 1.9, 145 to 186 tiles a frame): band energy median 4.0e-5 to 5.5e-5 at both tiers for 3804 to 3820 (2.6e-5 to 3.1e-5 at review in 3828, a fast frame, where the review tier spreads 16 samples over the shutter and so adds motion blur), fine over coarse 0.75 to 0.97 (0.57 to 0.66 in 3828 review). Flagged at review: 39, 36, 34, 40, 56 tiles of 451 to 459; 32 to 39 of them in one patch at tiles x 16 to 24, y 1 to 11, the lit side of the globe behind the limb, luma 0.90, fine 1.6e-5 and coarse 1.7e-5: flat at both scales, washed out by light, not a mosaic. The twelve tiles nearest the least report (x 29 to 31, y 8 to 13, report 1.00 to 1.09 in 3804 to 3820) carry 1.3e-5 to 6.2e-5 with ratio 0.42 to 1.06 at review; flagged among them: 2, 1, 0, 0 and 3 in 3828.

## MAIL 353 and 354 (2026-11-23 22:19 UTC): npm run smooth and npm run capture

Why: Brandon's second review note (kit/PROGRESS.md, section "Second review note of 2026-11-23", decision d147): lag at the change of view, things that appear at once, and he captures the film himself at 3840 by 2160 and 60 frames a second. The Builder gave me, for this: the `smooth` block in `config/showcase.json` (only that block), two lines in `package.json`, `tools/img/smooth.py`. Name them in every report.

Design as accepted (my answer of 22:17, MAIL 354 of 22:24):
- `npm run smooth -- --stop ID [--kind dive|rise] [--only path,pictures,place,counts] [--rate N] [--calibrate]`. Files `tools/smooth.ts`, `tools/smooth/path.ts` (pure, tested), `tools/img/smooth.py`. Writes `out/work/smooth/<stop>.json` and `.md`, exit 1 on any limit; `--calibrate` writes all and exits 0.
- (a) path, rate 2 (half frames through the director until travel_builder's `rate` lands), 12 film frames into each neighbour: log rate of height, turn of the camera, speed of the picture at the place, each over its mean; step over one film frame at most 0.08 (seams too), after the largest value no climb over 0.03, before it no fall over 0.03; a table per film frame (frame, rate over mean, step, turn, picture speed); judged at half frames as well.
- (b) pictures, draft, every frame of dive and rise and 12 film frames each side: level step against the middle of the two neighbours, pop score (residual after optical flow over the median of the 16 frames round it).
- (c) the handover report's numbers with the noplace probe. (d) counts per frame from the page (travel_builder's counter; assumed `showcase.counts()`), zero in dive, rise, travel; NOT MEASURED until it exists.
- Limits in `smooth` of the config; (a) set now, the rest null until the calibration on main (all nine stops, on the Builder's machines, job text from me), then only tightened.

MAIL 400 (Builder, from uranus_builder): the speed of the picture stepped by its own count of points (ring points passing into the sky near the horizon, count 8, 7, 6). Ruling: limits stay (0.08, 0.03); each step compares one fixed set of points, the count beside the number, fewer than 4 is "not measured" and red. Built:
- `tools/smooth/path.ts`: `judgeCurve(..., floor, members)` with `Members { values, min }` (what every point gave per capture frame). A step over one film frame and between capture frames is the mean over the points that have ground in both frames; a rebound pair over the points that have ground in every frame from the one to the other; the largest value of the move is sought over the points that have ground in every frame of the segment when at least 4 do. Each mark carries `points`; `CurveResult.points` (of, min, fewest, counts, notMeasured); fault rule `points` with the text NOT MEASURED where a judged step has fewer than 4. DECISION: a rebound pair with fewer than 4 common points is skipped, not red (the steps along it are judged and red when under 4).
- `tools/smooth/camera.ts`: `Curves.ring` (the values per point), `MIN_POINTS = 4`, and a second form of the ring, `ground` (8 points on a circle on the ground round the ground point under the place pixel, radius `ringPx / focal * distance`, at most 0.5 of the body radius), which never leaves the ground. `tools/smooth.ts`: option `--ring picture|ground` (default picture, the ruled form; ground is a proposal and has no config key), "over N points" beside worst step, half step, fall and climb, a points column in the per frame table.
- Tests: `tests/smooth.test.ts`, three new (a count of 8, 7, 6 does not step and the loose mean does; a rebound over the common points; fewer than 4 is red with NOT MEASURED). vitest 31 files, 239 tests pass; tsc clean; pyflakes clean.
- Proof on all nine stops (path part only, no browser), file `assets-src/shared/harness-cal/LIMITS.md`: red numbers of the speed curve 151 as in the nine jobs, 138 over the fixed set, 138 on the ground ring; no fault of rate or turn changes. The 13 that go: uranus 11 (dive whole frames 4 to 2, dive rate 2 6 to 2, rise 4 to 2, rise rate 2 7 to 4: the steps at 4179, 4181, 4398, 4400 are gone, worst step of the dive 0.193 at 4181 becomes 0.130 at 4122 over 8 points), jupiter rise at rate 2 12 to 10. Fewest points: uranus dive 6, uranus rise 5, jupiter rise 5, neptune rise 5, all others 8; no step is not measured.
- The ground ring moves numbers both ways: uranus dive climb after the largest value 0.262 (picture ring, over 8 points) against 0.003; neptune rise fall 0.045 against 0.145; mars rise fall 0.522 against 0.645; sun rise fall 0.027 against 0.059 (turns red); jupiter rise climb 0.191 against 0.159. MAIL 416 point 3, work and findings (2026-11-24, commit 56478329; files touched: `tools/smooth.ts`, `tools/img/smooth.py`, three keys in the `smooth` block of `config/showcase.json`):
- The frozen pictures: the Builder ran my job text (`scratch/mask/JOB-smooth-small.md`, jobs sms1-01 to 09 on 8c454593): each machine sent its frames at quarter size, 16 bit grey, value times 64. They sit in `scratch/small/small-<stop>/fNNNNN.png` (placed with `scratch/small/place.sh <stop>`). `python3 scratch/mask/all.py [stops]` gives the table from them, `scratch/mask/ssheet.py <stop> lo hi step cols out.png` a sheet to view. Poses of the frozen film: `scratch/mask/poses.json`. Neptune's dive I also hold as full draft frames in `repo/out/work/handover/neptune/frames` and ran the tool itself on them.
- (a) Neptune's dive, viewed: three changes of the look in place, none carried by pop, block pop or level step. 4685 to 4694 the dark oval storm of the far view turns into a detailed vortex with an eye; 4714 to 4722 the lower left darkens and white clouds appear at the eye; 4742 to 4750 the bright limb haze and the streaks fade to the near look and stars appear. The cut at T (4757 to 4758) is clean. Why the pop misses them: a dissolve of about 10 frames fills more than half of the 16 frame window, so the median is the dissolve.
- The new number, CHANGE IN PLACE: frame i against frame i + 8 film frames, carried back along the chained flows, the signed difference smoothed with a Gaussian of 20 master px, judged only where every flow on the way agrees with its flow back, where the pixel stays inside the frame, and where it starts on the body and arrives on the body (the unit sphere of the stop from the carried camera pose, less the blur at its edge). The number is the 99th percentile of the absolute value; the largest value, its pixel and the judged share stand beside it; under a judged share of 0.1 it is not measured. Config: `smooth.pictures.changeFrames` 8, `changeBlurPx` 20, `maxChange` null. Panel `<stop>-<kind>-changes.png`.
- Check on made pictures (`scratch/synth/check.py`): a texture at rest 0.0; the same texture sliding 12 px a frame 0.4; a soft patch of 40 display values coming in over 8 frames 38.9 at rest and 38.9 while sliding, and the pop of that is 0.77 and 1.02: the pop does not see it, the change does.
- The tool itself on the frozen Neptune dive (full frames, nothing rendered): level step 0.26 at 4744, pop 1.30 at 4716, block pop 7.89 at 4745, change 75.6 at 4743 (limb haze), 39.4 at 4735, 28.6 at 4711, 28.3 at 4688 (the storm). A steady fast dive without a change of look reads 10 to 13.6, a place at rest 1 to 4 (the Sun's boiling surface 11 to 13).
- (c) The mask: the pop is counted twice, with every pixel and with nothing left where the flow and the flow back do not agree (squared gap over 0.01 of the squared sizes plus 0.5 px squared at quarter size); the smaller is the pop, per block too (a block needs more than half of its pixels carried). Why not the mask alone: at the start of a move the stars are not carried but the two flows still agree while the motion is small, then they stop agreeing and the count drops, which reads as a pop that is not there (saturn dive 1.47 at 3544 with the mask alone, 1.08 without). Uranus dive 4140: 1.39 with every pixel, 1.12 carried only.
- (b) mars rise 2719 and 2720: a patch of ground changing in place, the film (1.31 stays). Jupiter: see below once its frames are here.

  - Definition: at the flow scale, on the frame and on the later frame carried back, under the judged mask of the change in place: detail = blur over 8 px minus blur over 40 px (master px, both normalised by the mask); local contrast = root of the pooled square of the detail over 40 px; number = 1 minus the pooled product of the two details over the product of the two contrasts (0 the same pattern, 1 unrelated, up to 2 reversed; it is half the pooled mean square of the difference of the two details each divided by its contrast). Judged only where both contrasts are over 1 display value and half of the pool is judged; the 99th percentile; not measured under a judged share of 0.02.

Not touched: packs, rubrics, jury texts, judged list; no critic receives anything different.
- BUILT, `tools/round.py` only (helpers `smooth_job`, `smooth_refuse`, `smooth_take`, `smooth_close`, `smooth_md`, `smooth_issues`, `smooth_say`, `smooth_line`, `hashes_now`; flags `--no-smooth-job`, `--smooth PATH`, `--smooth-minutes` 30).
- THE SWITCH is the key `smooth` in the round's state (`{'stops': [...], 'job': true|false}`). `jobs` writes it (stop round: its stop; record: every stop; polish: the stops of `--stops`); `place --smooth PATH` writes it for a stop round or a record round that has none. A round whose state has no key (every round whose `jobs` ran before this commit, the open round 41 Venus too) places and closes as before, byte for byte. To judge smooth in round 41 without sending jobs again: `place --round 41 --smooth out/work/smooth/venus.json` (or `jobs --round 41 --no-smooth-job`, with the same flags as the first time since it writes `jobs.json` again).
- `jobs`: one job a stop more, `r<N>-<stop>-smooth`: `npm run smooth -- --stop X --calibrate && npm run smooth -- --stop X --kind landing --calibrate` (both exit 0 with `--calibrate`), before it `rm -f` of the stop's reports and sheets, the tar holds `X.json X.md X-landing.json X-landing.md X-*.png` of `out/work/smooth/`. `--no-smooth-job` leaves the job out and keeps the judging on. Film rounds: 30 min a job by `--smooth-minutes`, an estimate that was never measured on a worker.
- `place`: after the tars, for each stop of the key, the report at `--smooth PATH` (a file, or a folder with `<stop>.json`) or else `out/work/smooth/<stop>.json` (where the job's tar and the Builder's own checks both land). Accepted only if `engineHash` and `stopHash` equal the round's and it is the full report (`only` holds the five parts, `kinds` dive and rise, `rate` the rate of its limits); the commit may differ. An accepted report is copied to `out/work/rounds/round-NNN/smooth/` with its `.md` and, when it sits beside it with the same commit, its landing report, so a later smooth run cannot overwrite it. A refused one is named with the reason and a report placed before stays.
- `close` (stop, polish, record; not whole film rounds): for each stop of the key the landing is measured there and then (`npm run -s smooth -- --stop X --kind landing --calibrate`) if `computeHashes` of the tree gives the round's engine and stop hash; if the tree is another build, the landing report of the job is used when one was placed, else the landing is not measured. Copied into the scorecard folder: `smooth-X.json`, `smooth-X.md`, `smooth-X-landing.json`, `smooth-X-landing.md` (no sheets). SUMMARY.md gets `## Smooth` (stop round after the measures, polish after the measures, record after the stops' bar sentence, so it also lands under "Final scores" of PROGRESS): one row a stop with report green, red or not measured, landing the same, the count of red lines, the first two lines and where each report was made. Each `why` line of both reports is one issue `N. smooth <stop>, severe, <file>: "<line>"` after the critics' severe issues, counted in the severe totals (stop round tail, polish, record board and totals). Pass: a stop holds only with a full report of the build placed and green and a landing measured and green; a stop round's tail says "Measures not green". Polish has no bar, as before. The console names five lines a stop and points to SUMMARY.md.
- DECISION: not measured is not a pass (as a measure that is missing today). DECISION: the limits are not compared with the config, the engine hash covers `config/` already. DECISION: the state key, not the round number, decides, so no open round changes under the Builder's hands.
- PROOF, all scratch in the clone, no render: (1) rounds 901 (stop), 921 (record), 931 (polish) closed by main's `round.py` (3060b72) and by the new one from the same restored inputs: 134 files (scorecards, scratch PROGRESS, state, console) equal, `diff -r` empty. - HOW I MERGE NOW: a depth 1 fetch leaves main without its history here, so `git merge` refuses (unrelated histories). The merge commit is made as `git merge --no-ff --no-commit -s ours --allow-unrelated-histories origin/main`, then `git read-tree --reset -u origin/main`, then `git checkout HEAD -- <my files that differ from main>`, then the commit. Before it: `git diff --stat <last merged main> origin/main -- <my files>` must be empty (main did not touch them), else those files are merged by hand. Two parents, main's tree plus my files, no force, no rebase.
- Merged main b0035c5c (status 223) that way at 08:43 UTC: commit 11c6c8d3, differs from main in this notebook only.

MAIL 580 (Builder, about 08:35 UTC): the pictures part of the smooth report on several machines. Asked: a slice of a move's window (`--part I/N` or `--frames A-B`) that renders the frames it owns plus the overlap and writes its own `.md`, `.json` and panels; a join that reads the N `.json` and writes the whole run's `.md` and `.json` with the same VERDICT and RED lines and names missing parts, without frames; every number of every owned frame exactly the whole run's; proof on Mercury's dive on main, whole against 4 parts joined; no limit, measure or judging change; no file outside `tools/` and `tests/`; keep the frame cache.
- ANSWERED at once (08:41): received, NAMED by 10:45 UTC; every row number can be sliced exactly; an inner slice renders 33 capture frames beyond its own; three things of a joined report cannot equal the whole run's (the two panel lines, the seconds a frame, the machine).
- WHAT A ROW NEEDS (read from `pictures()` in `tools/img/smooth.py`), g the frame's index in the window of N capture frames, w = popWindow times rate (32), reach = changeFrames times rate (16): the level step needs g - 1 and g + 1; the pop needs what is left between j and j + 1 for every j of `span(g, N, w)` (lo = max(min(g - w / 2, N - 1 - w), 0), hi = min(lo + w, N - 1)), so the frames lo to hi + 1; the change in place and the pattern need the frames g to g + reach and the flows between. Nothing reaches over the whole move. At the two ends of the window the median's window moves inward, so a slice must compute it on the whole window's indices: it is told its own first index and N.
- BUILT (commit 702c3d4c on `agent/harness`): `tools/smooth/slice.ts` (new: `popSpan` the twin of `span()`, `partOwn`, `sliceNeed`, `sliceName`, `missing`), `tools/smooth.ts` (`--part`, `--frames`, `--join`; `picturesOf` split into the render and measure, `sumsOf` (largest values, RED lines, the frames of the two panels, the longest run without a pattern, the ground beside the move: the same code for a whole run, a slice and the join) and `kindOf`; `readParts`, `joinPart`), `tools/img/smooth.py` (`pictures` takes `first`, `total`, `own`; `near()` places the median's window on the whole row and stops with an error when the slice does not hold it; pop and change are computed for owned frames only), `tests/smooth.test.ts` (6 new tests, 39 in the file). No `package.json` change.
- A SLICE: `npm run smooth -- --stop X --kind dive|rise --only pictures --part I/N [--calibrate]` (or `--frames A-B`, capture frames, both included). Part I owns the window indices floor((I - 1) N / parts) to floor(I N / parts) - 1. It renders lo to hi of `sliceNeed` (inner slice: 16 before, 17 after), through the same `still()`, so the frame cache is used as it is. Writes `out/work/smooth/<stop>-<kind>-pIofN.md`, `.json`, `-pops.png`, `-changes.png` (`-kA-B` for `--frames`). The `.json` is the whole run's shape with the owned rows only plus a top key `slice` (`name, part, of, total, own, need` in capture frames, `pops` and `changes` the frames its panels show, `rendered`, `seconds`). The `.md` says in its first paragraph that the largest values, the reds and the verdict are of these frames alone.
- THE JOIN: `npm run smooth -- --stop X --kind K --only pictures --join [FOLDER] [--calibrate]` (default folder `out/work/smooth`), reads every `<stop>-<kind>-pIofN.json` and `-kA-B.json` there, opens no page. DECISION: it judges by the limits the parts carry (the config of the commit that was measured), not by the config of the tree it runs in; `commit`, `engineHash`, `stopHash` are the parts'. DECISION: the parts must agree in commit, engine hash, stop hash, capture rate, limits, tier, page rate, segment, window, frame count and note, else exit 2 naming the two files and the field (tools are not in the engine hash, so the commit is compared too). A frame owned by two parts must have the same row in both, else exit 2. DECISION: a frame no part owns makes the pictures part not measured: the `.md` and `.json` are written with one RED line a gap (naming the missing part numbers), VERDICT red, exit 2 whatever `--calibrate` says.
- WHAT A JOINED REPORT CANNOT HAVE: the two panels of the whole move (no frames here): `panel` and `changePanel` are null and the two lines name, for each frame the whole run would draw, the part's panel that shows it. The four largest pops are always in their parts' panels. A peak of change can be in none (a part picks its own peaks at least 8 film frames apart): the line says so and names the slice that draws it (`--frames K-K`). The seconds a frame are the mean over the parts' rendered frames, the machine is the list of the parts' machines, a line names the parts, and the `.json` has a top key `joined`.
- CHECK without a render (synthetic frames, 90 of 640 by 360, a jump, a level step and a dissolve in them): the new `smooth.py` gives the old one's reply to the byte for a whole list; slices of 1, 2, 3, 4 and 7 parts give 8100 numbers of 18 kinds, 0 different from the whole list.
- PROOF on Mercury's dive (window 678 to 791, 228 draft capture frames at rate 2; tree of main b0035c5c plus this work; scratch `scratch/r580/`): (1) the whole run by main's own code (started before any edit; its commit line reads 11c6c8d3 with changes, since the tree was being edited when it ended), 228 frames rendered, 4.48 s a frame; (2) the whole run by 702c3d4c from the same frame cache: 229 table lines equal as printed, the rows of the `.json` equal to the last digit, the two panels equal to the byte, the `.md` differs in the commit line and the seconds line only; (3) the frame cache deleted, then parts 1 to 4 of 4 at 702c3d4c one after the other (74, 57, 57 and 40 frames rendered, the overlap of a part found in the cache of the one before; 291, 236, 347 and 192 s of rendering), then the join. Whole run against the join: 229 table lines equal as printed, 228 rows of the `.json` equal to the last digit, the one RED line equal (the pattern, 1.57 from 744 + 1/2, 48 frames over 0.45, first 712 + 1/2), VERDICT red in both; `diff` of the two `.md`: 8 lines (3 of the whole run, 5 of the join): the seconds line, the line that names the parts and its blank line, and the two panel lines. So the frames a part renders by itself give the same bytes as the whole run's on this machine. Parts 1 and 4 alone are green, 2 and 3 red: a part's verdict is of its frames alone.

## Not covered

- No run at the final or review tier, so the cost model (4.3 s per sample, a fast frame four times a slow one) is not checked against a real worker; `--sample-seconds` moves it.
- The worker was a second clone on the same VM, the tars went through the attachments folder by a script (`/home/user/workspace/scratch/run_jobs.py`, not in the repo). No real second VM, no real critic.
- The real mode of the film steps (round line in `kit/PROGRESS.md`, commit, push to main, `scorecards/`) was not run; it goes through the same `commit()`, `PROG` and `SC` as a stop round, which scratch mode swaps.
- A stop's measure is one job of about 20 min; the stress clip of `mars.dive` is one job of about 17 min at the review tier.
- The record round judges main as it is; it does not mirror the stops' branches.
- `--plan` leaves an empty cache folder for the round.
- Part 3: the cost model of `master.py` is not checked at review or final on a real worker; `--sample-seconds` moves it and `status` shows each worker's pace against the plan. Fit is judged on the busiest worker, not on the sum.
- Part 3: attachments are matched by file name, so two plans of the same tier and format that are out at the same time are told apart only by their records (build, tier, format, frames).
- Part 3: `clip` always writes `out/work/clips/<stop>.mp4`, whatever the tier; the move into `out/clips/` stays the Builder's step.
- Part 3: `gauntlet.py --deep` was run only against a stand in film, not against a real master. Without `--run gate`, items 11, 19 and 20 are read from the gate's last file and say NOT RUN when it is older than the last commit. Item 18 needs a sign in to the app URL and stays NOT RUN from this machine. The fade in rule (90 percent), the black thresholds and the 40 dB at the cut are the tool's own numbers.
- Part 3: no `cut/CUES.md` is committed.

MAIL 649 (11:35), the crash `tools/smooth.ts:396 TypeError ... reading 'toFixed'` (Uranus's rise part 4/4 on builder/proof-un2 05b61274).
- CAUSE: `changed()` in `tools/img/smooth.py` gives a change only where a tenth of the frame is judged (JUDGED_SHARE 0.1), `patterned()` gives a pattern from a fiftieth on (PATTERN_SHARE 0.02). A row with a small body in the frame has a pattern and change null. `sumsOf` takes the two largest patterns into `peaks` without asking for a change, and the label of the changes panel wrote `row.change!.toFixed(1)`. Such a row BELONGS among the peaks: the pattern is a judged number and the panel is its picture. So the fault is the label's.
- CURE (files: `tools/smooth/path.ts`, `tools/smooth.ts`, `tests/smooth.test.ts`): `peakRows(rows, apart)` (the choice of the rows, word for word what `sumsOf` did) and `changeNote(row)` (writes "change not measured" where it is null) in `tools/smooth/path.ts`; `tools/smooth.ts` calls both. Two tests in `tests/smooth.test.ts` ("the rows of the panel of changes"): both fail on the old code (2 failed of 45, checked by stashing the two tool files), 45 of 45 pass with the cure. `npx tsc --noEmit` exit 0.
- A FULL RUN can die the same way: `picturesOf` is the one function of a whole run and of a slice; it dies when one of the two largest pattern rows of the window has no change. `--join` cannot: it builds no label. `--kind landing` has the path part only (line 87 refuses any other): it cannot. No other `!.toFixed` in the file can meet a null: the pops panel's rows have pop, and pop implies left and base in `pictures()`.
- DISK: the tool refuses to start under 3 GB free. Freed: the k frames of every scratch folder except `ms1-marsdive`, `main-mercury`, `main-saturn` (their `cache-*.pkl` with a, later, ok are kept, so every candidate can still be read on every case; the null frames are kept), and `hw` is now a sparse checkout like `repo` (`/*`, `!/out/clips/`, `!/out/posts/`). 3.7 GB free at 11:49.
- RUNNING 11:49: the slice itself on 05b61274 in `repo` with its own tool (log `scratch/r623/uz2-old.log`), to see the crash here; then the cure laid over that tree (`git -C hw diff -- tools/smooth.ts tools/smooth/path.ts | git -C repo apply`), the same line again (frames cached), then one slice with old and new tool to show the rows are the same.
- PROVEN 11:57 (in `repo` on 05b61274, draft, rate 2): (1) with its own tool the line `npm run smooth -- --stop uranus --kind rise --only pictures --part 4/4` dies here as on the machine (line 396, after 60 of 66 frames, 236 s); (2) with the cure laid over that tree (same two changes; the import line merged by hand, un2's tool is older than main's) it ends green and writes `uranus-rise-p4of4.json`, `.md`, `-changes.png` (copies `scratch/r623/uz2-p4of4-*`). The row that killed it: capture 8915 (4457 + 1/2), change null (0.089 of the frame judged, under 0.1), pattern 0.071 (0.087 judged), the second of the two largest patterns; its label now reads "change not measured, pattern 0.07". (3) `--frames 8884-8899` with the old tool and with the cure: the 16 rows, the kind, the slice and both panels are the same to the byte (the .md differs in the commit line only), and the rows are those of part 4.

- `span(i, n, window, lead, tail)` in smooth.py and its twin `popSpan` in slice.ts: the row's ends lie lead frames before place 0 and tail after place n - 1, so with half a window at each end the window of every judged frame is i - 16 to i + 16 capture frames. `popContext(first, end, frameCount, popWindow)` gives the film frames of context: half a window, less where the film itself ends. `sliceNeed(..., lead, tail)` reaches into the context for the slices that own the ends.

The ground within about 130 px of it is clear in the first frame and lies under the horizon's haze in the later one: level +21 to +34, its own pattern dimmed.
- NUMBERS by distance from that border at 4910 (round the worst pixel, after plane out): detail of a 0.8 to 1.1 rms, of b 0.6 to 1.4, their correlation 0.09 to 0.6. Of b's detail the smooth level alone (a plane fit of width 40 px, read by the measure) carries 0.3 to 0.8, the rest (the dimmed ground) 0.4 to 0.7: under the floor on its own, over it together with the level's shape. The rests of a and b correlate 0.14 to 0.48.
- So part of the red IS the level's curve. But it is not the cut of the windows: whole windows read a curve fully. A second order border model (the level's quadratic continued across the border) therefore reads HIGHER at all nine starts (0.58 to 0.70 with the stable fits, against 0.44 to 0.61 today); plane out reads the curve less at a border than the measure reads it inside.

What stays red there is what the measure is defined to read (detail between 8 and 40 px): a level whose own shape is 50 to 100 px wide comes on over a dimmed pattern. MAIL 673 (13:36 UTC): job nx2-01 on a machine drew Neptune's texel frames 4820 to 4828 each on its own page: all code 0, 109 to 124 s each, free memory steady. So frame 4824 is not the cause; the cause of ne4-13 is what a page or browser holds or suffers over a long pass, unknown beyond that ("if you can show it, say so, if not, the cure stands without it"). ONE THING MORE: a texel frame of Neptune's surface view takes about 110 s on a machine, the whole G13 pass about 8 hours: the measure is wanted in parts. `npm run measure -- --stop ID --part I/N` draws only its share of the G13 frames (every frame in exactly one part) and writes a part file; G5 and G17 belong to one named part or a run of their own. `npm run measure -- --stop ID --join FOLDER` reads the parts, refuses parts that do not match each other or the build (commit, engine hash, stop hash, format, tier, rule, frame list), refuses a missing or doubled frame, writes the same `out/work/measure/<stop>.json` and prints the same lines as a whole run. Proof: on Venus the joined file equals the whole run to the digit (every field but the machine and the time). POINT 9 DESIGN (decided 13:30 to 13:45 UTC), files `tools/measure.ts`, `tools/measure/pages.ts`, `tests/pages.test.ts` (and for the parts `tools/measure/parts.ts`, `tests/parts.test.ts`); `tools/lib/frames.ts` not touched.
- `Keeper` in `pages.ts` (replaces `freshPage` of point 8, whose behaviour is `Keeper.fresh`): `draw(frame, fn)`; on a `PageFailure` prints "restart i of N at frame F on a new page: reason", closes the old page (waits at most 20 s for it), opens a new one, sets the view again, draws the same frame again; at most N a run (`--restarts`, default 3, printed in the first line); `--page-frames K` a fresh page every K frames (0 never); used by G13, the finals and `--carried`.
- Record `out/work/measure/<stop>.progress.jsonl` (ends in `.jsonl`: the gate lists `*.json` of that folder): first line the key (engine hash, stop hash, format, assets, browser, machine, noCover, texel tier and view, finals view, the `detail` block of the config, sha256 of `tools/measure/texels.ts`), then one line a finished frame (`g13` row; `final` row with tier, invalid, raw coverage, sha256 of the G17 png). A final is taken up only when its tier matches and, where G17 needs the png, the png on disk has the recorded sha256; `g17.py` then runs again on it. A record whose key differs in any name is not taken up (said, new record started); a torn last line is dropped; `--fresh` ignores a record; the record is removed at a good end. `--carried` has the restart but no take up (a handful of frames).
- Test hooks: `--close-page-at F[,F]` closes the page 300 ms into the frame's draw (the true path of ne4-13: the page call fails in flight), `--kill-browser-at F` kills the browser's process.
- The progress lines of G13 now also say what the browser holds (resident MB of its processes by `ps`) and the machine's free memory, so the next long job on a machine shows what the cause was.
- Facts of how a run went go under one key `run` of the json (restarts, pages, taken up, finals page), so "equal but for machine, seconds and run" is the comparison.
- Parts (2): `--part I/N` takes the picked frames whose place in the list is I - 1 modulo N (interleaved: the surface frames cost 30 times the hold's, blocks would be uneven), `--part finals` is `--only G5,G17` as a part; part files `out/work/measure/parts/<stop>-part-I-of-N.json`; `--join FOLDER` needs no browser.
- Probe `scratch/r669/probe.mts` on Neptune's proof 5fb44713 here (texel view, draft, one page): places 0 to 149 (the hold) flat: browser 2847 to 2869 MB (drawing process 1957, page 433 to 450), js heap 253 to 265 MB, 3.1 s a frame. The dive and the surface view follow.

- THE RULE NOW: the change is frame T in a dive, T+1 in a rise. A move without a place picture: half of the sheet before the change, half from it on (dive T-6 to T+5, rise T-5 to T+6). ## MAIL 745 (20:44 UTC): the pattern reader, my half starts now (replaces "stand by" of 743)
The law (the Builder's mail 741 to globe_engineer, word for word):
- "1. THE WAY. One law, in this order: (a) the pair is first carried by the camera's own map taken from a depth picture of the render, the body's turn taken out (so relief, an oblate body and the place's ground follow the same law; no unit sphere, no mask by shape); the sky by the camera's rotation alone; (b) the flow then runs on the carried pair and finds only what is left (a moon, a ring, a drifting cloud, a layer with height); (c) the reading is taken after both. A pair across the change of view needs both poses in one frame of the ground. What is fixed to the screen (the Sun's flare and bloom) stays with the flow."
- "3. THE REPORT. Each row carries the share of its judged pixels under each rule and which rule holds the row's largest block; new keys travel through part files."
- "4. THE LIMIT. 0.45 stays the number on main's reader until the proving run is in. The new reader's limit is 0.45 times the smallest ratio of new to main over the true exchanges of the proving set if that is under 1, else 0.45. Never above 0.45. I set the number from your table, you propose it."
- "5. THE PROVING RUN, before anything is trusted: (a) made cases with known truth: clean dives and rises at each enlargement globe_engineer used (must read under the limit) and true exchanges at each size (must read over it); (b) every dive and rise of the film on the batch tip 1924a104ed41def8574811c54b4d92ccb7e3febd, three columns a row: main's reader, globe_engineer's cure 780020f6, the new way; (c) every row where the new way and main's reader fall on different sides of their limit: the two frames as a picture with one line saying what is seen, sent to me (at most 10 a message)."

- THE READER AS BUILT: (1) the map of frame i onto frame j from i's depth, body by body (`X_j = place_j + turn_j (turn_i^-1 (X - place_i))`), the sky by the camera's rotation (the pose's `sky` quaternion when it has one); (2) read on the NEARER frame (the one the map draws the other larger on by the median of the map's slopes; the first when neither), the farther from its finer picture (levels of half octaves up to the full size, down to an eighth); (3) `seen`: the carried point arrives on its own surface (body equal, range within SAME 0.02 of the 3 by 3 neighbourhood of the arrival's depth), else it is covered; (4) one DIS flow each way on the carried pair; where they agree the pair is carried once more along map and flow; where they do not the map stands alone (so an exchange cannot go silent on the body, as it does on main); (5) rules per pixel: the map (seen, a body, the flow moved it at most FLOW_PX 0.5 reduced px = 2 px of the full size), the sky (seen, no body), the flow (the flow agrees and moved it more than FLOW_PX); covered and unmoved = not judged; (6) the rim: nothing judged within the change blur (5 reduced px, main's own margin) of the edge between surface and none in either depth picture, the frame's edge is no rim; (7) `pattern` and `patterned` of main, unchanged, on that.
- KEYS of a row: `patternMap`, `patternMapAt` (px of the full size of the frame read on), `patternMapShare`, `patternMapOn` (0 the first, 1 the later frame), `patternMapBy` {map, sky, flow} (shares of the judged pixels), `patternMapRule` (the rule of most pixels of the largest joined block at or over the row's value), `patternMapLeft` (99th percentile of the flow on what the map holds, px of the full size).

THE RULE: a line that says "of the second note" belongs to it; a line that says nothing belongs to the latest note that had begun at its round number, and a note begins with its lowest round whose line names it (the second note begins at round 42, so 43, 44, 46, 49 are of the second note and 37 to 41 of the first, as the Builder lists); without the flag `start` takes the latest note that has begun; the line names the note as soon as the phase knows a second one (a phase with one note writes the line as it always was, so a stop round of an earlier phase is byte equal). `k` in the state is now the round of the note; the critics' controls (`first = k == 1` at two places) read the new key `first` (the stop has no earlier round in the phase at all), so a first round of the second note does not collect controls again. Polish, whole film and record rounds count as before (line 969).
